Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for having me. I really hope I'm not wasting your time today. 

Um, I guess I'm here because I've just been feeling really worn down lately. Is it okay if I just explain a little bit of what's been going on? 

Well, for the past year or so, I've been trying to figure out—or, I guess, prove—that someone in the clinic system is changing or maybe holding back parts of my dad's lab results. I'm a phlebotomist, so I'm used to looking at these things, and... well, I just know the chart doesn't match what I originally saw. 

My family and all his doctors keep telling me I'm wrong and that his numbers are perfectly fine. I know it probably sounds silly, or like I'm being a difficult person, and I really don't want to be a bother to anyone... but it's just been so exhausting trying to get to the bottom of it. Am I explaining that okay?

Um... I really wish I could say it was just a gut feeling. That would be so much easier to just let go of, wouldn't it? But... no, it's based on what I actually saw.

I know it probably sounds silly, or like I'm being a difficult person, and I really don't want to be a bother to anyone... but it's just been so exhausting trying to get to the bottom of it. Am I explaining that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actually changing your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I... yes. I know I just mentioned it, and I really, really hate how it sounds. I don't want you to think I'm accusing anyone of being a bad person, because I know how hard everyone in the clinic works. I really do. \n\nBut... yes, I do think that. For the past year, no matter how many times my family or his doctors tell me his numbers are fine, I just can't let it go. Because I know what I saw, and the chart just doesn't match. I've been trying so hard to prove that someone is holding those parts back or changing them, and... it's just wearing me down to the bone. I'm so sorry if I sound completely unreasonable or difficult... am I making any sense at all?\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone is out to get you or your family, or is it just about the lab results?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I... I really hope I didn't give you that impression. I don't think anyone is out to get us at all. I know it probably sounds like I'm making these wild, awful accusations, and I promise I'm not. I don't think anyone is trying to hurt my dad or my family. \n\nIt's really just... just about the lab results. I just want the chart to be right. I don't think there's some big plot against us or anything scary like that. I just... I just think maybe someone altered that specific file and now they're just holding the real one back so they don't get in trouble at work? It's just that one specific thing with the chart. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer. I really don't want you to think I'm sitting here believing everyone is out to get me, because I know most people are really good. Does that clarify things a little bit?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results being altered is based on facts that you've seen, or is it more of a gut feeling that you can't shake?\n\nPatient: Um... I really wish I could say it was just a gut feeling. That would be so much easier to just let go of, wouldn't it? But... no, it's based on what I actually saw. \n\nI mean, because of my job, I look at these kinds of numbers all day, and I just know the chart in the system doesn't match the original results I saw with my own eyes. That's really why I've spent this whole past year trying so hard to prove that someone changed it or is holding the real one back. Even with my family and all his doctors constantly telling me the numbers are fine... I just can't unsee what I saw. \n\nTrying to figure it all out when everyone is telling me I'm wrong is just... it's really wearing me down to nothing. I know that probably makes me sound incredibly stubborn, and I really, really don't mean to be difficult or dismissive of the doctors, because I know they're experts. I just... I know what I saw, and I can't just pretend I didn't. Is it okay that I'm explaining it this way? I hope I'm not coming across as too argumentative.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that you might be mistaken about the lab results?\n\nPatient: I... I really, really have tried. I promise I have. I know how it looks, and I know how frustrating it must be for my family and the doctors to deal with me constantly questioning things. I don't want to be that person who thinks they know better than the professionals, because I really respect what you all do. \n\nAnd I have tried so hard to just tell myself that I'm wrong, that maybe I misread something or remembered it wrong. But... I just can't. For this whole past year, I've been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back those parts of his results. And no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nI know it sounds like I'm being impossible, and I'm so sorry. It's just wearing me down so much, and I wish I could just let it go. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm just being arrogant or refusing to listen... am I being too difficult by saying that?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your perception of the lab results might be incorrect due to a mistake on your part, rather than someone altering them?\n\nPatient: I... I know it probably seems like I'm just ignoring what you're saying, and I'm so sorry. I really don't mean to be. I ask myself that every single day. Because I work with these things, I know better than anyone that human error happens. I would love nothing more than to just admit I made a mistake, apologize, and move on. \n\nBut... I guess that's really why I'm here talking to you. Because for the past year, I’ve just been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And... and no matter how many doctors or family members sit me down and tell me the numbers are fine, I just know the chart doesn’t match what I saw. \n\nIt’s just wearing me down completely. I feel terrible that I can't just accept that I might be wrong, because I know how frustrating I must be to deal with. I really, truly want to be cooperative and listen to you. Am I making any sense at all, or am I just sounding completely unreasonable?\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the lab results were altered changed in intensity over the past year?\n\nPatient: I... well, I hope I'm not sounding like a broken record, since I know I keep bringing up the timeframe. But yes, this has all been going on for just about a year now. It really started right when my father’s medical problems began... you know, when he started requiring frequent labs and appointments. \n\nAs for the intensity... I don't know if it's gotten worse, exactly? I try really hard to keep my feelings in check so I don't overwhelm everyone around me. It's just been this constant, heavy worry ever since he got sick. I really want to be a good, supportive daughter and not make things harder for the medical team, so I try not to let it consume me. \n\nDo you think it's understandable that I've been so focused on this since his appointments started? I just really hope I'm not coming across as too obsessive... I just care about him so much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I... well, I have thought about it. I really have. I know getting a second opinion is a very normal, reasonable thing to do when you're worried about a loved one's health. And I would never want to seem like I don't trust the doctors we're seeing, because I know they work so hard and I appreciate them so much. 

But... I haven't actually taken him anywhere else. I guess I just... I worry that if I go to another doctor, they'll just look at the same system, the same charts that I know have been changed, and they'll just tell me the exact same thing. And then I'll just be wasting their time, too, which I really don't want to do. 

Plus, I don't want to put my dad through more appointments than he already has to go to. He's so tired, and I just want to be a good daughter and make things easier for him, not drag him all over town because I can't let this go. 

I know I probably should have asked someone else by now, though. I'm sorry, I know that's probably what I was supposed to do. Do you think I made the wrong choice by just staying at our current clinic? I really hope I haven't messed up his care by being so fixated on this one thing.

Am I explaining that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actually changing your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I... yes. I know I just mentioned it, and I really, really hate how it sounds. I don't want you to think I'm accusing anyone of being a bad person, because I know how hard everyone in the clinic works. I really do. \n\nBut... yes, I do think that. For the past year, no matter how many times my family or his doctors tell me his numbers are fine, I just can't let it go. Because I know what I saw, and the chart just doesn't match. I've been trying so hard to prove that someone is holding those parts back or changing them, and... it's just wearing me down to the bone. I'm so sorry if I sound completely unreasonable or difficult... am I making any sense at all?\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone is out to get you or your family, or is it just about the lab results?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I... I really hope I didn't give you that impression. I don't think anyone is out to get us at all. I know it probably sounds like I'm making these wild, awful accusations, and I promise I'm not. I don't think anyone is trying to hurt my dad or my family. \n\nIt's really just... just about the lab results. I just want the chart to be right. I don't think there's some big plot against us or anything scary like that. I just... I just think maybe someone altered that specific file and now they're just holding the real one back so they don't get in trouble at work? It's just that one specific thing with the chart. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer. I really don't want you to think I'm sitting here believing everyone is out to get me, because I know most people are really good. Does that clarify things a little bit?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results being altered is based on facts that you've seen, or is it more of a gut feeling that you can't shake?\n\nPatient: Um... I really wish I could say it was just a gut feeling. That would be so much easier to just let go of, wouldn't it? But... no, it's based on what I actually saw. \n\nI mean, because of my job, I look at these kinds of numbers all day, and I just know the chart in the system doesn't match the original results I saw with my own eyes. That's really why I've spent this whole past year trying so hard to prove that someone changed it or is holding the real one back. Even with my family and all his doctors constantly telling me the numbers are fine... I just can't unsee what I saw. \n\nTrying to figure it all out when everyone is telling me I'm wrong is just... it's really wearing me down to nothing. I know that probably makes me sound incredibly stubborn, and I really, really don't mean to be difficult or dismissive of the doctors, because I know they're experts. I just... I know what I saw, and I can't just pretend I didn't. Is it okay that I'm explaining it this way? I hope I'm not coming across as too argumentative.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that you might be mistaken about the lab results?\n\nPatient: I... I really, really have tried. I promise I have. I know how it looks, and I know how frustrating it must be for my family and the doctors to deal with me constantly questioning things. I don't want to be that person who thinks they know better than the professionals, because I really respect what you all do. \n\nAnd I have tried so hard to just tell myself that I'm wrong, that maybe I misread something or remembered it wrong. But... I just can't. For this whole past year, I've been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back those parts of his results. And no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nI know it sounds like I'm being impossible, and I'm so sorry. It's just wearing me down so much, and I wish I could just let it go. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm just being arrogant or refusing to listen... am I being too difficult by saying that?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your perception of the lab results might be incorrect due to a mistake on your part, rather than someone altering them?\n\nPatient: I... I know it probably seems like I'm just ignoring what you're saying, and I'm so sorry. I really don't mean to be. I ask myself that every single day. Because for the past year, I’ve just been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. I feel terrible that I can't just accept that I might be wrong, because I know how frustrating I must be to deal with. I really, truly want to be cooperative and listen to you. Am I making any sense at all, or am I just sounding completely unreasonable?\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the lab results were altered changed in intensity over the past year?\n\nPatient: I... well, I hope I'm not sounding like a broken record, since I know I keep bringing up the timeframe. I guess I just... I worry that if I go to another doctor, they'll just look at the same system, the same charts that I know have been changed, and they'll just tell me the exact same thing. And then I'll just be wasting their time, too, which I really don't want to do. \n\nPlus, I don't want to put my dad through more appointments than he already has to go to. He's so tired, and I just want to be a good daughter and make things easier for him, not drag him all over town because I can't let this go. \n\nI know I probably should have asked someone else by now, though. I really do try to doubt it. I want to doubt it so badly, because I know how much easier it would make everything for everyone. I don't want to be a burden on the doctors or my family. I try to tell myself, 'Maybe I just made a mistake, maybe I'm just stressed about my dad being sick.' \n\nBut... and I'm so sorry, I know I've said this already, and I really don't want to sound like I'm not listening to you... but for this whole past year, I've just been so focused on trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his lab results. I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nIt's just wearing me down so much, and I feel so guilty for not being able to just let it go. I really want to be agreeable and just accept what everyone is saying. I hope you don't think I'm just being stubborn or difficult... I really am trying my best to be reasonable. Does that make sense, or am I just being too much?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking a second opinion from another medical professional about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I... well, I have thought about it. I really have. I know getting a second opinion is a very normal, reasonable thing to do when you're worried about a loved one's health. And I would never want to seem like I don't trust the doctors we're seeing, because I know they work so hard and I appreciate them so much. \n\nBut... I haven't actually taken him anywhere else. I guess I just... I worry that if I go to another doctor, they'll just look at the same system, the same charts that I know have been changed, and they'll just tell me the exact same thing. And then I'll just be wasting their time, too, which I really don't want to do. \n\nPlus, I don't want to put my dad through more appointments than he already has to go to. He's so tired, and I just want to be a good daughter and make things easier for him, not drag him all over town because I can't let this go. \n\nI know I probably should have asked someone else by now, though. I'm sorry, I know that's probably what I was supposed to do. Do you think I made the wrong choice by just staying at our current clinic? I'm so sorry. I know I'm probably disappointing you by saying this, and I know I've been saying the same thing over and over. It's just... I'm a phlebotomist, you know? I work with labs. I know what I saw before the numbers were changed in the system. And for this whole past year, I've just been trying so hard to prove that someone is holding back parts of his results. \n\nI know my family and the doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I feel so terrible that I can't just agree with them and smile and let it go. I really want to. But I know the chart doesn't match what I saw, and it's just... it's wearing me down so much. I'm so exhausted from it. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just being completely unreasonable or that I'm not trying to be cooperative. I really am trying. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I... I want to say yes. I really do. I know that's the right answer, the one that shows I have insight and that I'm being rational. And I swear to you, I try to find that possibility. I don't want to be this difficult person who just won't listen to reason. 

But... I'm so sorry, I just keep coming back to the same thing. I've spent this entire last year trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is altering or hiding parts of my dad's lab results. And I feel so guilty because my family and the doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I know I should just accept that and be happy. I really want to be happy about it. 

But I'm a phlebotomist. I know what I saw before the system was updated. I know the chart doesn't match. And it's just... it's wearing me down so much to carry this around, to be the only one who sees it. I don't want to be right, I really don't. But I can't unsee what I saw. 

I'm sorry, I know that's probably very frustrating to hear. I'm trying so hard to be open-minded, I really am. Do you think I'm just being completely impossible? I really don't want to be a problem patient for you.

I... oh, that's such a hard question. I really want to be the kind of person who listens to the experts. I respect doctors so much—you all go to school for so long and know so much more than I do. I promise I'm not trying to be arrogant or think I know better than everyone else.

I really want to be the kind of person who listens to the experts. I respect doctors so much—you all go to school for so long and know so much more than I do. I promise I'm not trying to be arrogant or think I know better than everyone else. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I might sound so stubborn saying this... I think I would still hold onto it. I'm so sorry. I know that's not what a good patient is supposed to say. \n\nIt's just that... for this whole past year, I've been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his results. And I've already had every doctor and all my family members tell me the numbers are fine. They all disagree with me already. But I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nI don't want to be fighting everyone on this, I really don't. It's wearing me down so much, and I hate feeling like I'm constantly disappointing my family and the medical team. But when you see something with your own eyes, especially when it's your dad's health... how do you just pretend you didn't see it? \n\nI hope you can understand where I'm coming from, even just a little bit. I'm not trying to be difficult, I swear. Does it make sense why I can't just let it go, even when everyone tells me I should?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your belief about the lab results would change if you were shown the original lab results again?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 33 / phlebotomist / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results, and no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, I know the chart doesn’t match what I saw and it’s wearing me down.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I... I would love to see them. I really would. If someone could show me the real, untouched results, I promise you I would look at them so carefully and I'd be so grateful. I want to be able to just look at them and say, 'Oh, okay, I was wrong, thank goodness.' 

But... the problem is, I don't know how they could show me the real ones now. I'm so sorry, I know I must sound like a broken record, and I really don't want to frustrate you. It's just... like I was saying, for this whole past year, I've been trying to prove that someone inside the clinic system is the one changing or holding back parts of my dad's results. So if someone just printed out the 'original' from the computer system today... I'd know it was already altered. 

I feel terrible even saying that out loud to you. I know how it sounds. My family and the other doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I know I'm supposed to just trust the printouts they give me. I feel so guilty that I can't just smile and accept it. But I know the chart doesn't match what I actually saw that day before everything was updated. 

It's wearing me down so, so much. I'm just exhausted from carrying this around. I really hope you don't think I'm just trying to make your job harder or that I'm looking for a fight. I would love nothing more than to just let it go. Am I making any sense at all? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you don't think I'm just being stubborn on purpose.

I really have. I know getting a second opinion is a very normal, reasonable thing to do when you're worried about a loved one's health. I'm sorry, I know that's probably what I was supposed to do. Do you think I made the wrong choice by just staying at our current clinic? I really hope I haven't messed up his care by being so fixated on this one thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results is something that might not be true, but you believe it anyway?\n\nPatient: I... I know it sounds like I should think that. I really, really wish I could just say, 'Yes, it's all in my head, I'm just being silly.' I'm so sorry. I know I'm probably disappointing you by saying this, and I know I've been saying the same thing over and over. It's just... I'm a phlebotomist, you know? I work with labs. I know what I saw before the numbers were changed in the system. And for this whole past year, I've just been trying so hard to prove that someone is holding back parts of his results. \n\nI know my family and the doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I feel so terrible that I can't just agree with them and smile and let it go. I really want to. But I know the chart doesn't match what I saw, and it's just... it's wearing me down so much. I'm so exhausted from it. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just being completely unreasonable or that I'm not trying to be cooperative. I really am trying. Does it make sense why it's so hard for me to just let it go, since I saw it with my own eyes? I just really hope I'm not making you upset by sticking to this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that you could be mistaken about the lab results?\n\nPatient: I... I want to say yes. I really do. I know that's the right answer, the one that shows I have insight and that I'm being rational. And I swear to you, I try to find that possibility. I guess that's really why I'm here talking to you. Because for the past year, I’ve just been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And... and no matter how many doctors or family members sit me down and tell me the numbers are fine, I just know the chart doesn’t match what I saw. \n\nIt’s just wearing me down completely. I feel terrible that I can't just accept that I might be wrong, because I know how frustrating I must be to deal with. I really, truly want to be cooperative and listen to you. Am I making any sense at all, or am I just sounding completely unreasonable?\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the lab results were altered changed in intensity over the past year?\n\nPatient: I... well, I hope I'm not sounding like a broken record, since I know I keep bringing up the timeframe. But yes, this has all been going on for just about a year now. It really started right when my father’s medical problems began... you know, when he started requiring frequent labs and appointments. \n\nAs for the intensity... I don't know if it's gotten worse, exactly? I try really hard to keep my feelings in check so I don't overwhelm everyone around me. It's just been this constant, heavy worry ever since he got sick. I really want to be a good, supportive daughter and not make things harder for the medical team, so I try not to let it consume me. \n\nDo you think it's understandable that I've been so focused on this since his appointments started? I just really hope I'm not coming across as too obsessive... I just care about him so much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you doubted your belief that the lab results were altered?\n\nPatient: I... I really do try to doubt it. I want to doubt it so badly, because I know how much easier it would make everything for everyone. I don't want to be a burden on the doctors or my family. I try to tell myself, 'Maybe I just made a mistake, maybe I'm just stressed about my dad being sick.' \n\nBut... and I'm so sorry, I know I've said this already, and I really don't want to sound like I'm not listening to you... but for this whole past year, I've just been so focused on trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his lab results. And I feel awful saying this, because I know the doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, but I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nIt's just wearing me down so much, and I feel so guilty for not being able to just let it go. I really want to be agreeable and just accept what everyone is saying. I hope you don't think I'm just being stubborn or difficult... I really am trying my best to be reasonable. Does that make sense, or am I just being too much?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking a second opinion from another medical professional about your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I... well, I have thought about it. I really have. I know getting a second opinion is a very normal, reasonable thing to do when you're worried about a loved one's health. And I would never want to seem like I don't trust the doctors we're seeing, because I know they work so hard and I appreciate them so much. \n\nBut... I haven't actually taken him anywhere else. I guess I just... I worry that if I go to another doctor, they'll just look at the same system, the same charts that I know have been changed, and they'll just tell me the exact same thing. And then I'll just be wasting their time, too, which I really don't want to do. \n\nPlus, I don't want to put my dad through more appointments than he already has to go to. He's so tired, and I just want to be a good daughter and make things easier for him, not drag him all over town because I can't let this go. \n\nI know I probably should have asked someone else by now, though. I'm sorry, I know that's probably what I was supposed to do. Do you think I made the wrong choice by just staying at our current clinic? I really hope I haven't messed up his care by being so fixated on this one thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results is something that might not be true, but you believe it anyway?\n\nPatient: I... I know it sounds like I should think that. I really, really wish I could just say, 'Yes, it's all in my head, I'm just being silly.' It would make everything so much easier for the doctors, for my dad, for you... and I hate being the person who causes trouble or makes people frustrated. \n\nBut I... I just can't say it's not true. I'm so sorry. I know I'm probably disappointing you by saying this, and I know I've been saying the same thing over and over. It's just... I'm a phlebotomist, you know? I work with labs. I know what I saw before the numbers were changed in the system. And for this whole past year, I've just been trying so hard to prove that someone is holding back parts of his results. \n\nI know my family and the doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I feel so terrible that I can't just agree with them and smile and let it go. I really want to. But I know the chart doesn't match what I saw, and it's just... it's wearing me down so much. I'm so exhausted from it. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just being completely unreasonable or that I'm not trying to be cooperative. I really am trying. Does it make sense why it's so hard for me to just let it go, since I saw it with my own eyes? I just really hope I'm not making you upset by sticking to this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that you could be mistaken about the lab results?\n\nPatient: I... I want to say yes. I really do. I know that's the right answer, the one that shows I have insight and that I'm being rational. And I swear to you, I try to find that possibility. I don't want to be this difficult person who just won't listen to reason. \n\nBut... I'm so sorry, I just keep coming back to the same thing. I've spent this entire last year trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is altering or hiding parts of my dad's lab results. And I feel so guilty because my family and the doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I know I should just accept that and be happy. I really want to be happy about it. \n\nBut I'm a phlebotomist. I know what I saw before the system was updated. I know the chart doesn't match. And it's just... it's wearing me down so much to carry this around, to be the only one who sees it. I don't want to be right, I really don't. But I can't unsee what I saw. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably very frustrating to hear. I'm trying so hard to be open-minded, I really am. Do you think I'm just being completely impossible? I really don't want to be a problem patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results is something that you would still hold onto even if all evidence and experts disagreed with you?\n\nPatient: I... oh, that's such a hard question. I really want to be the kind of person who listens to the experts. I respect doctors so much—you all go to school for so long and know so much more than I do. I promise I'm not trying to be arrogant or think I know better than everyone else. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I might sound so stubborn saying this... I think I would still hold onto it. I'm so sorry. I know that's not what a good patient is supposed to say. \n\nIt's just that... for this whole past year, I've been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his results. And I've already had every doctor and all my family members tell me the numbers are fine. They all disagree with me already. But I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nI don't want to be fighting everyone on this, I really don't. It's wearing me down so much, and I hate feeling like I'm constantly disappointing my family and the medical team. But when you see something with your own eyes, especially when it's your dad's health... how do you just pretend you didn't see it? \n\nI hope you can understand where I'm coming from, even just a little bit. I'm not trying to be difficult, I swear. Does it make sense why I can't just let it go, even when everyone tells me I should?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results would change if you were shown the original lab results again?\n\nPatient: I... I would love to see them. I really would. If someone could show me the real, untouched results, I promise you I would look at them so carefully and I'd be so grateful. I want to be able to just look at them and say, 'Oh, okay, I was wrong, thank goodness.' \n\nBut... the problem is, I don't know how they could show me the real ones now. I'm so sorry, I know I must sound like a broken record, and I really don't want to frustrate you. It's just... like I was saying, for this whole past year, I've been trying to prove that someone inside the clinic system is the one changing or holding back parts of my dad's results. So if someone just printed out the 'original' from the computer system today... I'd know it was already altered. \n\nI feel terrible even saying that out loud to you. I know how it sounds. My family and the other doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I know I'm supposed to just trust the printouts they give me. I feel so guilty that I can't just smile and accept it. But I know the chart doesn't match what I actually saw that day before everything was updated. \n\nIt's wearing me down so, so much. I'm just exhausted from carrying this around. I really hope you don't think I'm just trying to make your job harder or that I'm looking for a fight. I would love nothing more than to just let it go. Am I making any sense at all? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you don't think I'm just being stubborn on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results would change if everyone, including your family and all the doctors, agreed that you were mistaken?\n\nPatient: I... I feel terrible saying this, because I know I must be testing your patience, and I really, really don't want to do that. But... they actually already do. \n\nThat's kind of where we are right now. For this whole past year, while I've been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his results... literally everyone, my family, all his doctors, they all already agree that I'm mistaken. They tell me over and over that the numbers are fine. And I try so hard to just smile and agree with them, because I want to be a good daughter and a good patient for you all. I don't want to cause trouble.\n\nBut... I just can't make myself forget what I saw. The chart just doesn't match. And it is wearing me down to absolutely nothing, having everyone I love look at me like I'm... well, like I'm doing something wrong, when I'm just trying to protect my dad. \n\nI know I sound completely unreasonable, don't I? I'm so sorry. I really hope you aren't upset with me for not being able to just agree with everyone. I promise I'm not trying to be difficult... do you think I'm just being awful to my family by holding onto this?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I... I'm not entirely sure how to answer that. I mean... I don't really think about it that way? 

I know I should probably say that it would be a huge relief, right? That I'd be so happy to know I was wrong and that my dad is perfectly fine and the clinic system is working exactly as it should. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I... I don't know. I really wish I could just say yes, absolutely, I'll accept it right now. I know that's what everyone wants from me, and I hate being the person who's dragging this out. I really do. 

But... it's just that I saw what I saw. For a whole year now, I've been trying so hard to show people that someone in the system is messing with his results, or holding things back. And I know how exhausting it is for my family, and for the doctors, to keep telling me the numbers are fine while I keep saying the chart doesn't match. 

It's just... it's wearing me down so much. I want to be agreeable. I want to just let it go and be normal again. But how do you just pretend you didn't see something that you know is real, especially when it's about your dad's health? I feel so awful even admitting that I can't just let it go. I'm sorry, I know I'm probably being exactly the kind of difficult patient you don't like dealing with. Am I making this too hard for you? I really don't mean to.

It's just that one specific thing with the chart. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer. I really don't want you to think I'm sitting here believing everyone is out to get me, because I know most people are really good. Does that clarify things a little bit?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results being altered is based on facts that you've seen, or is it more of a gut feeling that you can't shake?\n\nPatient: Um... I really wish I could say it was just a gut feeling. That would be so much easier to just let go of, wouldn't it? But... no, it's based on what I actually saw. \n\nI mean, because of my job, I look at these kinds of numbers all day, and I just know the chart in the system doesn't match the original results I saw with my own eyes. That's really why I've spent this whole past year trying so hard to prove that someone changed it or is holding the real one back. Even with my family and all his doctors constantly telling me the numbers are fine... I just can't unsee what I saw. \n\nTrying to figure it all out when everyone is telling me I'm wrong is just... it's really wearing me down to nothing. I know that probably makes me sound incredibly stubborn, and I really, really don't mean to be difficult or dismissive of the doctors, because I know they're experts. I just... I know what I saw, and I can't just pretend I didn't. Is it okay that I'm explaining it this way? I hope I'm not coming across as too argumentative.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that you might be mistaken about the lab results?\n\nPatient: I... I really, really have tried. I promise I have. I know how it looks, and I know how frustrating it must be for my family and the doctors to deal with me constantly questioning things. I don't want to be that person who thinks they know better than the professionals, because I really respect what you all do. \n\nAnd I have tried so hard to just tell myself that I'm wrong, that maybe I misread something or remembered it wrong. But... I just can't. For this whole past year, I've been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back those parts of his results. And no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nI know it sounds like I'm being impossible, and I'm so sorry. It's just wearing me down so much, and I wish I could just let it go. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm just being arrogant or refusing to listen... am I being too difficult by saying that?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your perception of the lab results might be incorrect due to a mistake on your part, rather than someone altering them?\n\nPatient: I... I know it probably seems like I'm just ignoring what you're saying, and I'm so sorry. I really don't mean to be. I ask myself that every single day. Because I work with these things, I know better than anyone that human error happens. I would love nothing more than to just admit I made a mistake, apologize, and move on. \n\nBut... I guess that's really why I'm here talking to you. Because for the past year, I’ve just been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And... and no matter how many doctors or family members sit me down and tell me the numbers are fine, I just know the chart doesn’t match what I saw. \n\nIt’s just wearing me down completely. I feel terrible that I can't just accept that I might be wrong, because I know how frustrating I must be to deal with. I really, truly want to be cooperative and listen to you. Am I making any sense at all, or am I just sounding completely unreasonable?\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the lab results were altered changed in intensity over the past year?\n\nPatient: I... well, I hope I'm not sounding like a broken record, since I know I keep bringing up the timeframe. But yes, this has all been going on for just about a year now. It really started right when my father’s medical problems began... you know, when he started requiring frequent labs and appointments. \n\nAs for the intensity... I don't know if it's gotten worse, exactly? I try really hard to keep my feelings in check so I don't overwhelm everyone around me. It's just been this constant, heavy worry ever since he got sick. I really want to be a good, supportive daughter and not make things harder for the medical team, so I try not to let it consume me. \n\nDo you think it's understandable that I've been so focused on this since his appointments started? I just really hope I'm not coming across as too obsessive... I just care about him so much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you doubted your belief that the lab results were altered?\n\nPatient: I... I really do try to doubt it. I want to doubt it so badly, because I know how much easier it would make everything for everyone. I don't want to be a burden on the doctors or my family. I try to tell myself, 'Maybe I just made a mistake, maybe I'm just stressed about my dad being sick.' \n\nBut... and I'm so sorry, I know I've said this already, and I really don't want to sound like I'm not listening to you... but for this whole past year, I've just been so focused on trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his lab results. And I feel awful saying this, because I know the doctors and my family keep telling me the numbers are fine, but I just... He's so tired, and I just want to be a good daughter and make things easier for him, not drag him all over town because I can't let this go. \n\nI know I probably should have asked someone else by now, though. I... oh, I really want to give you a good answer for this. I want to say yes, that if you just showed me the right proof, I'd drop all of this and apologize for taking up everyone's time for the last year. 

But... I just don't know what that would even look like. Because I saw it. I saw what I saw, and for a whole year I've been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad's lab results. And I know how that sounds... I know I must sound so stubborn and difficult. 

I mean, literally everyone—my family, all his doctors—they've all told me the numbers are fine. And I really, really want to believe them. I want to be the kind of person who just listens to the doctors and doesn't cause trouble. But the chart just doesn't match what I saw. 

It's just wearing me down so much... trying to protect him but feeling like everyone is disappointed in me. I'm sorry, I know that's not the answer you were hoping for. I'm probably being so frustrating, aren't I? Is it okay that I just can't see a way to change my mind right now?

I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever be able to accept that the lab results are accurate, even if it means going against what you believe you saw?\n\nPatient: I... I really have. I know getting a second opinion is a very normal, reasonable thing to do when you're worried about a loved one's health. And I would never want to seem like I don't trust the doctors we're seeing, because I know they work so hard and I appreciate them so much. \n\nBut... I haven't actually taken him anywhere else. I guess I just... I worry that if I go to another doctor, they'll just look at the same system, the same charts that I know have been changed, and they'll just tell me the exact same thing. And then I'll just be wasting their time, too, which I really don't want to do. \n\nPlus, I don't want to put my dad through more appointments than he already has to go to. He's so tired, and I just want to be a good daughter and make things easier for him, not drag him all over town because I can't let this go. \n\nI know I probably should have asked someone else by now, though. I'm sorry, I know that's probably what I was supposed to do. Do you think I made the wrong choice by just staying at our current clinic? I really hope I haven't messed up his care by being so fixated on this one thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results is something that might not be true, but you believe it anyway?\n\nPatient: I... I know it sounds like I should think that. I really, really wish I could just say, 'Yes, it's all in my head, I'm just being silly.' It would make everything so much easier for the doctors, for my dad, for you... and I hate being the person who causes trouble or makes people frustrated. \n\nBut I... I just can't say it's not true. I'm so sorry. I know I'm probably disappointing you by saying this, and I know I've been saying the same thing over and over. It's just... I'm a phlebotomist, you know? I work with labs. I know what I saw before the numbers were changed in the system. And for this whole past year, I've just been trying so hard to prove that someone is holding back parts of his results. \n\nI know my family and the doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I feel so terrible that I can't just agree with them and smile and let it go. I really want to. But I know the chart doesn't match what I saw, and it's just... it's wearing me down so much. I'm so exhausted from it. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just being completely unreasonable or that I'm not trying to be cooperative. I really am trying. Does it make sense why it's so hard for me to just let it go, since I saw it with my own eyes? I just really hope I'm not making you upset by sticking to this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that you could be mistaken about the lab results?\n\nPatient: I... I want to say yes. I really do. I know that's the right answer, the one that shows I have insight and that I'm being rational. And I swear to you, I try to find that possibility. I don't want to be this difficult person who just won't listen to reason. \n\nBut... I'm so sorry, I just keep coming back to the same thing. I've spent this entire last year trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is altering or hiding parts of my dad's lab results. And I feel so guilty because my family and the doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I know I should just accept that and be happy. I really want to be happy about it. \n\nBut I'm a phlebotomist. I know what I saw before the system was updated. I know the chart doesn't match. And it's just... it's wearing me down so much to carry this around, to be the only one who sees it. I don't want to be right, I really don't. But I can't unsee what I saw. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably very frustrating to hear. I'm trying so hard to be open-minded, I really am. Do you think I'm just being completely impossible? I really don't want to be a problem patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results is something that you would still hold onto even if all evidence and experts disagreed with you?\n\nPatient: I... oh, that's such a hard question. I really want to be the kind of person who listens to the experts. I respect doctors so much—you all go to school for so long and know so much more than I do. I promise I'm not trying to be arrogant or think I know better than everyone else. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I might sound so stubborn saying this... I think I would still hold onto it. I'm so sorry. I know that's not what a good patient is supposed to say. \n\nIt's just that... for this whole past year, I've been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his results. And I've already had every doctor and all my family members tell me the numbers are fine. They all disagree with me already. But I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nI don't want to be fighting everyone on this, I really don't. It's wearing me down so much, and I hate feeling like I'm constantly disappointing my family and the medical team. But when you see something with your own eyes, especially when it's your dad's health... how do you just pretend you didn't see it? \n\nI hope you can understand where I'm coming from, even just a little bit. I'm not trying to be difficult, I swear. Does it make sense why I can't just let it go, even when everyone tells me I should?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results would change if you were shown the original lab results again?\n\nPatient: I... I would love to see them. I really would. If someone could show me the real, untouched results, I promise you I would look at them so carefully and I'd be so grateful. I want to be able to just look at them and say, 'Oh, okay, I was wrong, thank goodness.' \n\nBut... the problem is, I don't know how they could show me the real ones now. I'm so sorry, I know I must sound like a broken record, and I really don't want to frustrate you. It's just... like I was saying, for this whole past year, I've been trying to prove that someone inside the clinic system is the one changing or holding back parts of my dad's results. So if someone just printed out the 'original' from the computer system today... I'd know it was already altered. \n\nI feel terrible even saying that out loud to you. I know how it sounds. My family and the other doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I know I'm supposed to just trust the printouts they give me. I feel so guilty that I can't just smile and accept it. But I know the chart doesn't match what I actually saw that day before everything was updated. \n\nIt's wearing me down so, so much. I'm just exhausted from carrying this around. I really hope you don't think I'm just trying to make your job harder or that I'm looking for a fight. I would love nothing more than to just let it go. Am I making any sense at all? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you don't think I'm just being stubborn on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results would change if everyone, including your family and all the doctors, agreed that you were mistaken?\n\nPatient: I... I feel terrible saying this, because I know I must be testing your patience, and I really, really don't want to do that. But... they actually already do. \n\nThat's kind of where we are right now. But... no, it's based on what I actually saw. \n\nI mean, because of my job, I look at these kinds of numbers all day, and I just know the chart in the system doesn't match the original results I saw with my own eyes. That's really why I've spent this whole past year trying so hard to prove that someone changed it or is holding the real one back. Even with my family and all his doctors constantly telling me the numbers are fine... I just can't unsee what I saw. \n\nTrying to figure it all out when everyone is telling me I'm wrong is just... it's really wearing me down to nothing. I know that probably makes me sound incredibly stubborn, and I really, really don't mean to be difficult or dismissive of the doctors, because I know they're experts. I just... I know what I saw, and I can't just pretend I didn't. Is it okay that I'm explaining it this way? I hope I'm not coming across as too argumentative.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that you might be mistaken about the lab results?\n\nPatient: I... I really, really have tried. I promise I have. I know how it looks, and I know how frustrating it must be for my family and the doctors to deal with me constantly questioning things. I don't want to be that person who thinks they know better than the professionals, because I really respect what you all do. \n\nAnd I have tried so hard to just tell myself that I'm wrong, that maybe I misread something or remembered it wrong. But... I just can't. For this whole past year, I've been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back those parts of his results. And no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nI know it sounds like I'm being impossible, and I'm so sorry. It's just wearing me down so much, and I wish I could just let it go. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm just being arrogant or refusing to listen... am I being too difficult by saying that?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your perception of the lab results might be incorrect due to a mistake on your part, rather than someone altering them?\n\nPatient: I... I know it probably seems like I'm just ignoring what you're saying, and I'm so sorry. I really don't mean to be. I ask myself that every single day. Am I making any sense at all, or am I just sounding completely unreasonable?\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the lab results were altered changed in intensity over the past year?\n\nPatient: I... well, I hope I'm not sounding like a broken record, since I know I keep bringing up the timeframe. It really started right when my father’s medical problems began... you know, when he started requiring frequent labs and appointments. \n\nAs for the intensity... I really want to be a good, supportive daughter and not make things harder for the medical team, so I try not to let it consume me. \n\nDo you think it's understandable that I've been so focused on this since his appointments started? I just really hope I'm not coming across as too obsessive... I just care about him so much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you doubted your belief that the lab results were altered?\n\nPatient: I... I really do try to doubt it. I try to tell myself, 'Maybe I just made a mistake, maybe I'm just stressed about my dad being sick.' \n\nBut... and I'm so sorry, I know I've said this already, and I really don't want to sound like I'm not listening to you... but for this whole past year, I've just been so focused on trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his lab results. I really have. I know getting a second opinion is a very normal, reasonable thing to do when you're worried about a loved one's health. And I would never want to seem like I don't trust the doctors we're seeing, because I know they work so hard and I appreciate them so much. \n\nBut... I haven't actually taken him anywhere else. I guess I just... I worry that if I go to another doctor, they'll just look at the same system, the same charts that I know have been changed, and they'll just tell me the exact same thing. He's so tired, and I just want to be a good daughter and make things easier for him, not drag him all over town because I can't let this go. \n\nI know I probably should have asked someone else by now, though. I know what I saw before the numbers were changed in the system. And for this whole past year, I've just been trying so hard to prove that someone is holding back parts of his results. \n\nI know my family and the doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I feel so terrible that I can't just agree with them and smile and let it go. I want to say yes. I really do. I know that's the right answer, the one that shows I have insight and that I'm being rational. And I swear to you, I try to find that possibility. I don't want to be this difficult person who just won't listen to reason. \n\nBut... I'm so sorry, I just keep coming back to the same thing. And I feel so guilty because my family and the doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I know I should just accept that and be happy. I really want to be happy about it. \n\nBut I'm a phlebotomist. I know what I saw before the system was updated. I know the chart doesn't match. And it's just... it's wearing me down so much to carry this around, to be the only one who sees it. I don't want to be right, I really don't. But I can't unsee what I saw. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably very frustrating to hear. I'm trying so hard to be open-minded, I really am. Do you think I'm just being completely impossible? I really don't want to be a problem patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results is something that you would still hold onto even if all evidence and experts disagreed with you?\n\nPatient: I... oh, that's such a hard question. I really want to be the kind of person who listens to the experts. I respect doctors so much—you all go to school for so long and know so much more than I do. I promise I'm not trying to be arrogant or think I know better than everyone else. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I might sound so stubborn saying this... I think I would still hold onto it. I'm so sorry. I know that's not what a good patient is supposed to say. \n\nIt's just that... for this whole past year, I've been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his results. And I've already had every doctor and all my family members tell me the numbers are fine. They all disagree with me already. But I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nI don't want to be fighting everyone on this, I really don't. It's wearing me down so much, and I hate feeling like I'm constantly disappointing my family and the medical team. But when you see something with your own eyes, especially when it's your dad's health... how do you just pretend you didn't see it? \n\nI hope you can understand where I'm coming from, even just a little bit. I'm not trying to be difficult, I swear. Does it make sense why I can't just let it go, even when everyone tells me I should?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results would change if you were shown the original lab results again?\n\nPatient: I... I would love to see them. I really would. If someone could show me the real, untouched results, I promise you I would look at them so carefully and I'd be so grateful. I want to be able to just look at them and say, 'Oh, okay, I was wrong, thank goodness.' \n\nBut... the problem is, I don't know how they could show me the real ones now. I'm so sorry, I know I must sound like a broken record, and I really don't want to frustrate you. It's just... like I was saying, for this whole past year, I've been trying to prove that someone inside the clinic system is the one changing or holding back parts of my dad's results. My family and the other doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I know I'm supposed to just trust the printouts they give me. I feel so guilty that I can't just smile and accept it. I really hope you don't think I'm just trying to make your job harder or that I'm looking for a fight. I would love nothing more than to just let it go. Am I making any sense at all? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you don't think I'm just being stubborn on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results would change if everyone, including your family and all the doctors, agreed that you were mistaken?\n\nPatient: I... I feel terrible saying this, because I know I must be testing your patience, and I really, really don't want to do that. But... they actually already do. \n\nThat's kind of where we are right now. For this whole past year, while I've been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his results... literally everyone, my family, all his doctors, they all already agree that I'm mistaken. They tell me over and over that the numbers are fine. And I try so hard to just smile and agree with them, because I want to be a good daughter and a good patient for you all. I don't want to cause trouble.\n\nBut... I just can't make myself forget what I saw. The chart just doesn't match. And it is wearing me down to absolutely nothing, having everyone I love look at me like I'm... well, like I'm doing something wrong, when I'm just trying to protect my dad. \n\nI know I sound completely unreasonable, don't I? I'm so sorry. I really hope you aren't upset with me for not being able to just agree with everyone. I promise I'm not trying to be difficult... do you think I'm just being awful to my family by holding onto this?\n\nDoctor: How much distress do you feel when you think about the possibility that the lab results might not be altered?\n\nPatient: I... I know it probably seems like I'm just ignoring what you're saying, and I'm so sorry. I really don't mean to be. I ask myself that every single day. Because for the past year, I’ve just been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. Am I making any sense at all, or am I just sounding completely unreasonable?\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the lab results were altered changed in intensity over the past year?\n\nPatient: I... well, I hope I'm not sounding like a broken record, since I know I keep bringing up the timeframe. I just really hope I'm not coming across as too obsessive... I just care about him so much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you doubted your belief that the lab results were altered?\n\nPatient: I... I really do try to doubt it. I try to tell myself, 'Maybe I just made a mistake, maybe I'm just stressed about my dad being sick.' \n\nBut... and I'm so sorry, I know I've said this already, and I really don't want to sound like I'm not listening to you... but for this whole past year, I've just been so focused on trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his lab results. And it's just... it's wearing me down so much to carry this around, to be the only one who sees it. I don't want to be right, I really don't. But I can't unsee what I saw. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably very frustrating to hear. I'm trying so hard to be open-minded, I really am. Do you think I'm just being completely impossible? I really don't want to be a problem patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results is something that you would still hold onto even if all evidence and experts disagreed with you?\n\nPatient: I... oh, that's such a hard question. I really want to be the kind of person who listens to the experts. I respect doctors so much—you all go to school for so long and know so much more than I do. I promise I'm not trying to be arrogant or think I know better than everyone else. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I might sound so stubborn saying this... I think I would still hold onto it. I'm so sorry. I know that's not what a good patient is supposed to say. \n\nIt's just that... for this whole past year, I've been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his results. And I've already had every doctor and all my family members tell me the numbers are fine. They all disagree with me already. But I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. \n\nI don't want to be fighting everyone on this, I really don't. It's wearing me down so much, and I hate feeling like I'm constantly disappointing my family and the medical team. But when you see something with your own eyes, especially when it's your dad's health... how do you just pretend you didn't see it? \n\nI hope you can understand where I'm coming from, even just a little bit. I'm not trying to be difficult, I swear. Does it make sense why I can't just let it go, even when everyone tells me I should?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the lab results would change if you were shown the original lab results again?\n\nPatient: I... I would love to see them. I really would. If someone could show me the real, untouched results, I promise you I would look at them so carefully and I'd be so grateful. I want to be able to just look at them and say, 'Oh, okay, I was wrong, thank goodness.' \n\nBut... the problem is, I don't know how they could show me the real ones now. I'm so sorry, I know I must sound like a broken record, and I really don't want to frustrate you. So if someone just printed out the 'original' from the computer system today... I'd know it was already altered. \n\nI feel terrible even saying that out loud to you. I know how it sounds. My family and the other doctors keep telling me the numbers are fine, and I know I'm supposed to just trust the printouts they give me. I feel so guilty that I can't just smile and accept it. I'm not entirely sure how to answer that. I mean... I don't really think about it that way? \n\nI know I should probably say that it would be a huge relief, right? That I'd be so happy to know I was wrong and that my dad is perfectly fine and the clinic system is working exactly as it should. And I really, really want to feel that way. I want to be someone who just trusts the system and doesn't cause all this worry for everyone. \n\nBut... honestly, I don't really spend time thinking about the possibility that they aren't altered, because... well, because of what I saw. It's just so real to me. So I guess I don't really feel distress about the *possibility* of them being normal, because my mind just doesn't go there. The distress is all from... well, from knowing what I saw and having no one believe me. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably not a very helpful answer. Does that make sense at all? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever be able to accept that the lab results are accurate, even if it means going against what you believe you saw?\n\nPatient: I...
